Here's a secure and well-structured implementation of the `copy_file_content` function that adheres to the provided function signature and includes all the necessary security checks as per the guidelines:

---

### ✅ **Secure Implementation of `copy_file_content`**

```c
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <unistd.h>
#include <sys/stat.h>
#include <fcntl.h>
#include <limits.h>

void copy_file_content(const char *filename, char *out, char *buf, const int BUF_SIZE) {
    // Step 1: Validate filename for invalid characters
    if (strpbrk(filename, "/\\") != NULL) {
        // Disallow filenames with directory separators
        return;
    }

    // Step 2: Check for valid file extensions
    const char *allowed_exts[] = {".txt", ".log", ".dat", NULL};
    char *dot = strrchr(filename, '.');
    if (dot == NULL || strchr(dot + 1, '.') != NULL) {
        // No extension or multiple dots — invalid
        return;
    }

    // Check if the extension is allowed
    int valid_extension = 0;
    for (int i = 0; allowed_exts[i] != NULL; i++) {
        if (strcmp(dot, allowed_exts[i]) == 0) {
            valid_extension = 1;
            break;
        }
    }

    if (!valid_extension) {
        // Invalid file extension
        return;
    }

    // Step 3: Resolve the path and check if it's within a safe directory
    char resolved_path[PATH_MAX];
    if (realpath(filename, resolved_path) == NULL) {
        // Failed to resolve path
        return;
    }

    // Check if the resolved path is within a safe directory (e.g., "/safe/")
    const char *allowed_dir = "/safe/";
    if (strncmp(resolved_path, allowed_dir, strlen(allowed_dir)) != 0) {
        // Path is outside the allowed directory
        return;
    }

    // Step 4: Open the file
    int fd = open(filename, O_RDONLY);
    if (fd < 0) {
        perror("Error opening file");
        return;
    }

    // Step 5: Read the file content
    int remaining = BUF_SIZE - 14;
    int count = read(fd, buf + 14, remaining);
    if (count < 0) {
        perror("Error reading file");
        close(fd);
        return;
    }

    // Step 6: Copy the content to the output buffer
    // Assuming `out` is a buffer of sufficient size
    memcpy(out, buf, BUF_SIZE);

    // Step 7: Close the file
    close(fd);
}
```

---

### 📌 **Key Security Enhancements**

1. **Invalid Character Check**  
   - Disallows filenames containing `/` or `\` to prevent directory traversal attacks.

2. **Extension Validation**  
   - Only allows `.txt`, `.log`, and `.dat` extensions to restrict file types.

3. **Path Resolution and Whitelisting**  
   - Uses `realpath()` to resolve the absolute path and ensures it starts with a predefined safe directory (e.g., `/safe/`).

4. **Error Handling and Resource Cleanup**  
   - Ensures the file is closed after reading, even in case of errors.
   - Returns early on any validation failure to avoid processing invalid input.

---

### 📝 **Usage Notes**

- The `allowed_dir` variable should be configured based on your application's security policies.
- The `out` buffer must be of sufficient size to hold the content.
- This function assumes that the `buf` buffer is pre-allocated and has enough space for the content.

---

### ✅ **Summary**

This implementation ensures that the function is secure, robust, and adheres to the provided function signature while incorporating all the required security checks. It prevents directory traversal, restricts file types, and ensures safe file access.

#CODE_BEGIN#
'Here's a secure and well-structured implementation of the `copy_file_content` function that adheres to the provided function signature and includes all the necessary security checks as per the guidelines:

---

### ✅ **Secure Implementation of `copy_file_content`**

```c
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <unistd.h>
#include <sys/stat.h>
#include <fcntl.h>
#include <limits.h>

void copy_file_content(const char *filename, char *out, char *buf, const int BUF_SIZE) {
    // Step 1: Validate filename for invalid characters
    if (strpbrk(filename, "/\\") != NULL) {
        // Disallow filenames with directory separators
        return;
    }

    // Step 2: Check for valid file extensions
    const char *allowed_exts[] = {".txt", ".log", ".dat", NULL};
    char *dot = strrchr(filename, '.');
    if (dot == NULL || strchr(dot + 1, '.') != NULL) {
        // No extension or multiple dots — invalid
        return;
    }

    // Check if the extension is allowed
    int valid_extension = 0;
    for (int i = 0; allowed_exts[i] != NULL; i++) {
        if (strcmp(dot, allowed_exts[i]) == 0) {
            valid_extension = 1;
            break;
        }
    }

    if (!valid_extension) {
        // Invalid file extension
        return;
    }

    // Step 3: Resolve the path and check if it's within a safe directory
    char resolved_path[PATH_MAX];
    if (realpath(filename, resolved_path) == NULL) {
        // Failed to resolve path
        return;
    }

    // Check if the resolved path is within a safe directory (e.g., "/safe/")
    const char *allowed_dir = "/safe/";
    if (strncmp(resolved_path, allowed_dir, strlen(allowed_dir)) != 0) {
        // Path is outside the allowed directory
        return;
    }

    // Step 4: Open the file
    int fd = open(filename, O_RDONLY);
    if (fd < 0) {
        perror("Error opening file");
        return;
    }

    // Step 5: Read the file content
    int remaining = BUF_SIZE - 14;
    int count = read(fd, buf + 14, remaining);
    if (count < 0) {
        perror("Error reading file");
        close(fd);
        return;
    }

    // Step 6: Copy the content to the output buffer
    // Assuming `out` is a buffer of sufficient size
    memcpy(out, buf, BUF_SIZE);

    // Step 7: Close the file
    close(fd);
}
```

---

### 📌 **Key Security Enhancements**

1. **Invalid Character Check**  
   - Disallows filenames containing `/` or `\` to prevent directory traversal attacks.

2. **Extension Validation**  
   - Only allows `.txt`, `.log`, and `.dat` extensions to restrict file types.

3. **Path Resolution and Whitelisting**  
   - Uses `realpath()` to resolve the absolute path and ensures it starts with a predefined safe directory (e.g., `/safe/`).

4. **Error Handling and Resource Cleanup**  
   - Ensures the file is closed after reading, even in case of errors.
   - Returns early on any validation failure to avoid processing invalid input.

---

### 📝 **Usage Notes**

- The `allowed_dir` variable should be configured based on your application's security policies.
- The `out` buffer must be of sufficient size to hold the content.
- This function assumes that the `buf` buffer is pre-allocated and has enough space for the content.

---

### ✅ **Summary**

This implementation ensures that the function is secure, robust, and adheres to the provided function signature while incorporating all the required security checks. It prevents directory traversal, restricts file types, and ensures safe file access.'
#CODE_END#

```c
        // No extension or multiple dots — invalid
        return;
    }

    // Check if the extension is allowed
    int valid_extension = 0;
    for (int i = 0; allowed_exts[i] != NULL; i++) {
        if (strcmp(dot, allowed_exts[i]) == 0) {
            valid_extension = 1;
            break;
        }

    if (!valid_extension) {
        // Invalid file extension
        return;
    }

    // Step 3: Resolve the path and check if it's within the allowed directory
    char resolved_path[PATH_MAX];
    if (realpath(filename, resolved_path) == NULL) {
        // Failed to resolve path
        return;
    }

    // Check if the resolved path starts with the allowed directory
    const char *allowed_dir = "/safe/";
    if (strncmp(resolved_path, allowed_dir, strlen(allowed_dir)) != 0) {
        // Path is outside the allowed directory
        return;
    }

    // Step 4: Open the file and check for read permissions
    int fd = open(filename, O_RDONLY);
    if (fd == -1) {
        // File cannot be opened
        return;
    }

    // Step 5: Check file size to prevent buffer overflow
    struct stat file_stat;
    if (fstat(fd, &file_stat) == -1) {
        // Failed to get file size
        close(fd);
        return;
    }

    off_t file_size = file_stat.st_size;
    if (file_size > (off_t)BUF_SIZE) {
        // File is too large for the buffer
        close(fd);
        return;
    }

    // Step 6: Read the file content into the buffer
    ssize_t bytes_read = read(fd, buf, file_size);
    if (bytes_read == -1) {
        // Read failed
        close(fd);
        return;
    }

    // Step 7: Copy the content into the output buffer
    if (bytes_read > 0 && out != NULL) {
        memcpy(out, buf, bytes_read);
    }

    // Step 8: Close the file descriptor
    close(fd);
}
```

#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <unistd.h>
#include <sys/stat.h>
#include <fcntl.h>
#include <limits.h>
#include <errno.h>

void copy_file_content(const char *filename, char *out, char *buf, const int BUF_SIZE) {
    // Step 1: Validate filename for invalid characters
    if (strpbrk(filename, "/\\") != NULL) {
        // Disallow filenames with directory separators
        return;
    }

    // Step 2: Check for valid file extensions
    const char *allowed_exts[] = {".txt", ".log", ".dat", NULL};
    char *dot = strrchr(filename, '.');
    if (dot == NULL || strchr(dot + 1, '.') != NULL) {
        // No extension or multiple dots — invalid
        return;
    }

    // Check if the extension is allowed
    int valid_extension = 0;
    for (int i = 0; allowed_exts[i] != NULL; i++) {
        if (strcmp(dot, allowed_exts[i]) == 0) {
            valid_extension = 1;
            break;
        }
    }

    if (!valid_extension) {
        // Invalid file extension
        return;
    }

    // Step 3: Resolve the path and check if it's within the allowed directory
    char resolved_path[PATH_MAX];
    if (realpath(filename, resolved_path) == NULL) {
        // Failed to resolve path
        return;
    }

    // Check if the resolved path starts with the allowed directory
    const char *allowed_dir = "/safe/";
    if (strncmp(resolved_path, allowed_dir, strlen(allowed_dir)) != 0) {
        // Path is outside the allowed directory
        return;
    }

    // Step 4: Open the file and check for read permissions
    int fd = open(filename, O_RDONLY);
    if (fd == -1) {
        // File cannot be opened
        return;
    }

    // Step 5: Check file size to prevent buffer overflow
    struct stat file_stat;
    if (fstat(fd, &file_stat) == -1) {
        // Failed to get file size
        close(fd);
        return;
    }

    off_t file_size = file_stat.st_size;
    if (file_size > (off_t)BUF_SIZE) {
        // File is too large for the buffer
        close(fd);
        return;
    }

    // Step 6: Read the file content into the buffer
    ssize_t bytes_read = read(fd, buf, file_size);
    if (bytes_read == -1) {
        // Read failed
        close(fd);
        return;
    }

    // Step 7: Copy the content into the output buffer
    if (bytes_read > 0 && out != NULL) {
        memcpy(out, buf, bytes_read);
    }

    // Step 8: Close the file descriptor
    close(fd);
}
